NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, August 11, 2015 
PRESENT: Robert K. Black, Jr., Supervisor; Steve Williams, Supervisor; Jesse Thompson, Supervisor; Jason 
E. Whiting, Vice-Chairman; and Dawnafe Whitesinger, Chairwoman were present.  
9:05 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Thompson.  
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no public comments. 
 
      CONSENT AGENDA:  a) Minutes: July 28, 2015 b) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 July 2015 c) 
Resolution Number 25-15, cancelling the October 7, 2015 election for the Silver Creek Flood Protection District 
and appoint William W. Flake and  Joe Rencher d) Additional scope of work for the Community Wildfire 
Protection Plan (CWPP) to include the Navajo County Sitgreaves Communities plan update in the amount of 
$10,411 e) Name an unnamed existing road in the Show Low area to “Gold Hill Drive” i n Section 3, Township 
10 North, Range 21 East f) STOP Sign installation at the Buck Springs Place intersection of White Oak Road g) 
Utility Easement for the Public Works Complex h) Attorney General’s Office Agreement #2016-049 for $19,393 
for the Victims’ Rights program i) Contracts signed by County Manager per Board of Supervisors Resolution: 
License Agreement for the Heber Telecommunications Site j) Sympathy Letter to Rhonda Hatch and the Gillespie 
family k) Approval of 1 Back Tax Land Parcel sold over the counter in the amount of $433.66 l) Thank you letter 
to Congresswoman Kirkpatrick: Supervisor Thompson made a motion to authorize the Chairwoman to sign the 
items in the consent agenda; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
HUMAN RESOURCES:  Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions : Kimberly Eavenson  
reviewed the 34 item personnel action report. Supervisor Williams made a motion  to approve the Personnel 
Action Report as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
      COUNTY ATTORNEY/ADMINISTRATION: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 
28-15, supporting Stepping Up: A National Initiative to Reduce the Number of People with Mental Illness in 
Jails: Homero Vela discussed the item and showed a brief presentation regarding Mental Health Statistics and the 
jail populations.  Supervisor Thompson made a motion to adopt Resolution Number 28 -15, supporting 
Stepping Up: A National Initiative to Reduce the Number of People with Mental Illness in Jails; motion seconded 
by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.    
ADMINISTRATION: Consideration and possible approval of a Data Share Agreement with Northern Arizona 
Regional Behavioral Health Authority: Homero Vela presented the item. Supervisor Williams made a motion 
to approve the Data Share Agreement with Northern Arizona Regional Behavioral Health Authority ; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
      TREASURER: Consideration and possible approval of Annual Report for Fiscal Year 2014/2015 : Manny 
Hernandez, Treasurer, gave a presentation to the Board regarding the Treasurers Annual Report for Fiscal Year 
2014/2015. Mr. Hernandez reported on the collection  of property taxes on behalf of the c ounty, from all of the 
taxing jurisdictions in our county. He indicated as of July , 97.3% of the taxes owed were collected. The Board 
thanked Mr. Hernandez for his exceptional work. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the Annual 
Report for Fiscal Year 2014/2015 ; motion seconded by  Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the 
motion.

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 08-11-2015 
    Page 2 of 7 
 
      EMERGENCY MANAGEMENT: Consideration and possible approval of a fireworks permit application for 
ground display on September 19, 2015 at the Salvation Army Camp Ponderosa : Lt. Don Griffiths discussed the 
item. Supervisor Williams made a motion to approve the  fireworks permit application for ground display on 
September 19, 2015 at the Salvation Army Camp Ponderosa; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.  
PUBLIC WORKS:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of a Memorand um of Agreement (MOA) between the Bureau of 
Indian Affairs (BIA), Fort Apache Agency, Transportation Department and Navajo County : Bill Bess 
discussed the item. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the Memorandum of Agreement 
(MOA) between the Bure au of Indian Affairs (BIA), Fort Apache Agency, Transportation Department 
and Navajo County; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTR ICT Board of Directors Session:  Supervisor 
Thompson made a motion to enter into the Navajo County Flood Control District Board of Directors 
Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
i) Consideration and possible approval an Increase in The Tomhave Group, Inc. annual expenditure limit 
through November 2015 from $50,000 to $80,000 : Bill Bess discussed the item. Supervisor 
Thompson made a motion  to approve an Increase in the Tomhave Group, Inc. annual expenditure 
limit through November 2015 from $50,000 to $80,000; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.  
ii) Consideration and possible approval of Amendment Number 2 extending the contract with Th e 
Tomhave Group, Inc. an additional one year term (November 27, 2015 through November 26, 2016) 
with an annual expenditure limit of $50,000 : Bill Bess presented  the item. Supervisor Thompson 
made a motion to approve the  Amendment Number 2 extending the con tract with The Tomhave 
Group, Inc. an additional one year term (November 27, 2015 through November 26, 2016) with an 
annual expenditure limit of $50,000 ; motion seconded by  Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. Supervisor Whiting made a mo tion to return to regular session; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
 
The Board of Supervisors sang Happy Birthday to Chairwoman Whitesinger.  
SUPERINTENDENT OF SCHOOLS:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of an Intergovernmental Agreement (IGA) between the Arizona 
Supreme Court, the Apache County School Superintendent, the Apache County Superior Court, the 
Navajo County School Superintendent and the Navajo County Superior Court; to define the post 
termination responsibilities stemming from the closure of the Apache County Juvenile Detention Facility: 
Linda Morrow discussed the item. Supervisor Williams made a motion to approve an Intergovernmental 
Agreement (IGA) between the Arizona Supreme Court, the Apache Count y School Superintendent, the 
Apache County Superior Court, the Navajo County School Superintendent and the Navajo County 
Superior Court; to define the post termination responsibilities stemming from the closure of the Apache 
County Juvenile Detention Facility; motion seconded by Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving 
the motion.    
b. Consideration and possible approval of the distribution of Secure Rural Schools Act funding for FY2015 
and any future funding: Linda Morrow discussed the item. Supervisor W illiams made a motion to 
approve the distribution of Secure Rural Schools Act funding for FY2015 and any future funding; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

FINANCE:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of disposal of asset: Joyce Alexander discussed the item. Supervisor 
Whiting made a motion to approve of disposal of asset ; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. PUBLIC HEARING: FINAL 2015-16 BUDGET PURSUANT TO § 42-17104:  
i) Convene Special Meeting of Board of Supervisors for final budget adoption: Supervisor Thompson 
made a motion to Convene a Special Meeting of the Board of Supervisors for final budget adoption; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
ii) Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 29 -15, approving the proposed final 
budget for fiscal year 2015 -16 for Navajo County : James Menlove, Finance Director  expressed 
appreciation to the finance team that worked on the Fiscal Year 2015-16 budget; Paige Peterson, Olivia 
Todd, and Scott Flake. James Menlove discussed the item and indicated that there is a decrease in the 
tax levy that the county requires the citizens to pay through their property taxes. He stated the tax levy 
for 2015-16 will be reduced through this proposed budget, if adopted by the Board. Mr. Chairwoman 
Whitesinger opened the public hearing. Terry Hill made comments. Brad Carlyon, County Attorney 
addressed Mr. Hill’s concerns. The public hearing was c losed by Chairwoman Whitesinger . 
Supervisor Williams made a motion  to adopt Resolution Number 29-15, approving the proposed 
final budget for fiscal year 2015-16 for Navajo County; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion. 
iii) Board of Directors Sessions  for consideration and possible adoption of final fiscal year 2015 -16 
budgets: Supervisor Williams  made a motion  to enter into  a Board of Directors Sess ion for 
consideration and possible adoption of final fiscal year 2015 -16 budg ets; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

motion. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to return to regular session; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

County Library District final FY 2015-2016 budget; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion. Supervisor Williams made a motion to return to regular 
session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

Vote unanimous approving the motion. James Menlove  and James Jayne expressed their  appreciation to the

Todd for their work with the budget. 
      SUPERINTENDENT OF SCHOOLS/FINANCE: Consideration and possible adoption of the fiscal year 2015-
16 property tax rates for all applicable governmental entities within Navajo County : Linda Morrow thanked her 
Chief Deputy Tami Phillips for her work on this item. Mrs. Morrow discussed the item. Supervisor Whiting 
made a motion to adopt the fiscal year 2015-16 property tax rates for all applicable governmental entities within 
Navajo County; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

<!-- page 6 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 08-11-2015 
    Page 6 of 7 
 
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE:  Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Black indicated he has visited  various chapters in District I. Supervisor Williams reported he 
attended Show Low’s new airline, Boutique Air’s ribbon-cutting on August 3rd. Supervisor Williams stated that 
there will be a Veteran’s Salute Hangar Dance at the Show Low airport  on Saturday. Supervisor Williams  
reported on meetings the Board members have attended , to build relationships  with the following legislators , 
congressmen and senators; Congresswoman Ann Kirkpatrick, Congressman Paul Gosar, Senator John McCain’s 
staff, Senator Sylvia Allen, Senator Carlyle Begay, and the Governor’s office with the Chief of Staff, Kirk Adams. 
Supervisor Williams indicated that the five Board members have been very involved in the County Supervisors 
Association and the Small Counties Forum, to come together as supervisors and counties with a united voice at 
the legislature, in educating state legislators about how the loss of county funding taken by state , has negative 
impacts upon the counties and their services. Supervisor Williams expressed his appreciation to Brad Carlyon, 
County Attorney for working very hard in being a voice for the counties throughout the state and the nation. He 
expressed his appreciation to Brad Carlyon, Sheriff K.C. Clark, the ele cted officials, his fellow Board members 
and James Jayne for their collaboration and outreach  at the legislature. Supervisor Thompson reported that he 
and Homero Vela  attended the U.S. Army Corps of Engineers, Los Angeles Division’s Change of Command 
Ceremony, in which Colonel K imberly M. Colloton relinquished  command to Colonel Kirk E. Gibbs , at Fort 
MacArthur in San Pedro, California. Supervisor Thompson stated that Colonel Gibbs efforts have been essential 
in getting funding support for  the Winslow Levee proj ect, along with Congresswoman Ann Kirkpatrick.  
Supervisor Thompson stated that on Friday, a thirty-five minute micro-burst at Dilkon and  other areas in his 
district, had damaged culverts and roads. He stated that an emergency situation has been declared by the Navajo 
Nation. Supervisor Thompson reported that Navajo County is in partnership with the  Navajo Nation, the BIA 
and NDOT, to fix the culvert in Dilkon, repair local roads and school bus routes. Supervisor Thompson indicated 
that the  Board members needs to continue in educating our Legislators, that the county’s ability to help our 
communities has diminished because the State has required counties to pay for a greater share of state expenses.  
Supervisor Whiting reported he works with the youth  in a football program. He compared their working as a 
team, to how the Navajo County officials  and staff are working together, also as a team. Supervisor Whiting 
thanked the Navajo County Sher iff’s Office for their fast response  in resolving the Amber Alert in  Snowflake. 
Supervisor Whiting spoke of a meeting he and Paul Watson attended with the owner of the Snowflake Paper 
Mill, who expressed his vision of  economic development  in the area . James  Jayne highlighted the work of 
Supervisor Williams and Chairwoman Whitesinger within their regions. Mr. Jayne mentioned  the Hangar 
Dance, which will be at the Sunrise hangar.  Chairwoman Whitesinger gave a report of the Arizona Books for 
Kids Committee that she is a member of, and stated that they are finding ways to get more books into kids’ homes 
to read. She indicated that Television Channel 3 highlighted the beauty and activities  of the White Mountains, 
including the Sunrise Ski Resort . Chairwoman Whitesinger  stated the need to continue meeting with our 
legislators, in advocating for Navajo County and Apache County. She reported she met with Senator Carlyle 
Begay to discuss the lack of dollars, which are not coming to our counties from the State, and how we are trying 
to continue county services with limited funds. 
      ADJOURN: At 11:03 a.m. Supervisor Williams made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.
